msg_e64b7aa8e001YdGKnA01HiM6KT|2026-05-26 14:36:58|assistant|Here's the full load analysis for `sumeetsaini.com` (cold cache):

### Core Web Vitals
| Metric | Value | Rating |
|--------|-------|--------|
| **LCP** | **404ms** | ✅ Excellent (threshold 2.5s) |
| **CLS** | **0.04** | ✅ Good (threshold <0.1) |
| **TTFB** | **5ms** | ✅ Incredible (Cloudflare edge) |

Despite great scores, there's room to improve perceived load.

### 3 Biggest Issues

**1. Font is the #1 LCP bottleneck (368ms)**
The custom font `ProFontIIx-subset.woff2` isn't discovered until `main.css` is downloaded and parsed. The browser can't render the title text until the font arrives, contributing **92% of the LCP delay**.
→ **Fix**: Add `<link rel="preload" href="/fonts/ProFontIIx-subset.woff2" as="font" crossorigin>` to the `<head>`. The browser will start downloading the font immediately, not after CSS parsing.

**2. Vulkan API in critical path (565ms)**
`home.js` makes a fetch to `vulkan.sumeetsaini.com/projects/` which takes 565ms. This is the longest chain in the critical path. `home.js` is a regular `<script>` tag at the bottom of body — technically not render-blocking for initial paint, but the 21 project images and 9 demo videos all begin loading immediately from the API response.
→ **Fix**: 565ms is a backend cold-start issue on the API. You could have `home.js` return a cached response from `sessionStorage` or a service worker. Not much you can do on the frontend since the API is already preconnected.

**3. 4-hour cache on everything**
All your static assets (JS, CSS, fonts, images) use `max-age=14400` (4 hours). On a return visit the browser still has to revalidate everything. `three.module.js` alone is ~570KB.
→ **Fix**: Bump to `max-age=31536000` (1 year) for versioned/fingerprinted assets. Currently nothing changes that often.

### What's already good
- **Deferred CSS**: `popup.css`, `projects.css`, `labels.css` loaded via `media="print"` trick — correct approach
- **Preconnect**: Both `vulkan.sumeetsaini.com` and `arcanecodex.dev` are preconnected — correct
- **No unnecessary render-blocking**: Only `main.css` and the inline `<script>` block rendering; `home.js` is at the bottom
- **60 network requests** is on the high side but they're all small

### Suggested action order
1. Preload the font — quickest win, biggest impact on LCP
2. Increase cache TTL for static assets
3. If you want to reduce total requests (60), lazy-load the project images/videos until the user enters the 3D scene

Want me to implement any of these?

}

```
1	const NAME = "Sumeet Saini";
2	const TYPE_SPEED = 100;
3	const CURSOR_PAUSE = 1300;
4	
5	// Prefetch projects and blog data in background for faster popup loading
6	// (Only fetches metadata — images/videos load on demand when user opens a project)
7	(function prefetchAPIs() {
8		fetch("https://vulkan.sumeetsaini.com/projects/")
9			.then((res) => res.json())
10			.then((data) => {
11				window._projectsCache = data;
12			})
13			.catch(() => {});
14	
15		fetch("https://arcanecodex.dev/index.json")
16			.then((res) => res.json())
17			.then((data) => {
18				window._blogCache = data;
19			})
20			.catch(() => {});
21	})();
22	
23	const nameEl = document.getElementById("name");
24	const textEl = nameEl.querySelector(".text");
25	const cursorEl = nameEl.querySelector(".cursor");
26	const content = document.getElementById("content");
27	
28	// Prevent page scrolling
29	document.body.style.overflow = "hidden";
30	document.documentElement.style.overflow = "hidden";
31	
32	// Track when typing is complete
33	let typingComplete = false;
34	
35	// TYPEWRITER
36	let idx = 0;
37	const typeInterval = setInterval(() => {
38		textEl.textContent += NAME[idx++];
39		if (idx === NAME.length) {
40			clearInterval(typeInterval);
41			typingComplete = true;
42		}
43	}, TYPE_SPEED);
44	
45	function moveToTop(container) {
46		cursorEl.classList.add("hide");
47	
48		// Safari iOS: fade out → move → fade in (bypasses position animation bugs)
49		const isSafariIOS =
50			(/iPhone|iPad|iPod/.test(navigator.userAgent) ||
51				(navigator.userAgent.includes("Mac") && "ontouchend" in document)) &&
52			!/Chrome|Android/.test(navigator.userAgent);
53	
54		if (isSafariIOS) {
55			// Fade out at current center position
56			nameEl.style.transition = "opacity 0.3s ease";
57			nameEl.style.opacity = "0";
58	
59			setTimeout(() => {
60				// Move to final position while hidden
61				nameEl.style.transition = "none";
62				nameEl.style.left = window.innerWidth <= 600 ? "1rem" : "2rem";
63				nameEl.style.top = window.innerWidth <= 600 ? "1rem" : "1.2rem";
64				nameEl.style.transform = "none";
65				nameEl.style.fontSize = window.innerWidth <= 600 ? "1.2rem" : "";
66	
67				// Fade in at final position (sync with shape)
68				requestAnimationFrame(() => {
69					nameEl.style.transition = "opacity 0.3s ease";
70					nameEl.style.opacity = "1";
71				});
72	
73				setTimeout(() => {
74					nameEl.style.transition = "";
75					content.classList.remove("hidden");
76					if (container) container.style.opacity = "1";
77				}, 300);
78			}, 300);
79			return;
80		}
81	
82		// Standard browsers: use CSS transition
83		nameEl.classList.add("top");
84		setTimeout(() => {
85			content.classList.remove("hidden");
86			if (container) {
87				container.style.opacity = "1";
88			}
89		}, 600);
90	}
91	
92	// Make function available globally for shape module to call
93	window.completeIntro = (container) => {
94		window._introComplete = true;
95		clearTimeout(window._slowTimer);
96		document.documentElement.classList.remove('slow-connection');
97		// Only proceed if typing is complete
98		if (typingComplete) {
99			moveToTop(container);
100		} else {
101			// If typing isn't complete, wait for it
102			const checkTyping = setInterval(() => {
103				if (typingComplete) {
104					clearInterval(checkTyping);
105					moveToTop(container);
106				}
107			}, 50);
108		}
109	};
110

54	function moveToTop(container) {
55		cursorEl.classList.add("hide");

57		// Safari iOS: fade out → move → fade in (bypasses position animation bugs)
58		const isSafariIOS =
59			(/iPhone|iPad|iPod/.test(navigator.userAgent) ||
60				(navigator.userAgent.includes("Mac") && "ontouchend" in document)) &&
61			!/Chrome|Android/.test(navigator.userAgent);
62	
63		if (isSafariIOS) {
64			// Fade out at current center position
65			nameEl.style.transition = "opacity 0.3s ease";
66			nameEl.style.opacity = "0";
67	
68			setTimeout(() => {
69				// Move to final position while hidden
70				nameEl.style.transition = "none";
71				nameEl.style.left = window.innerWidth <= 600 ? "1rem" : "2rem";
72				nameEl.style.top = window.innerWidth <= 600 ? "1rem" : "1.2rem";
73				nameEl.style.transform = "none";
74				nameEl.style.fontSize = window.innerWidth <= 600 ? "1.2rem" : "";
75	
76				// Fade in at final position (sync with shape)
77				requestAnimationFrame(() => {
78					nameEl.style.transition = "opacity 0.3s ease";
79					nameEl.style.opacity = "1";
80				});
81	
82				setTimeout(() => {
83					nameEl.style.transition = "";
84					content.classList.remove("hidden");
85					if (container) container.style.opacity = "1";
86				}, 300);
87			}, 300);
88			return;
89		}
90	
91		// Standard browsers: use CSS transition
92		nameEl.classList.add("top");

> AGENT

It was added in commit `6e2f218` (Apr 4, 2026) — the inline comment said it "bypasses position animation bugs" specific to iOS Safari. Simultaneously transitioning `left`, `top`, `transform`, and `font-size` on a `position: fixed` element used to glitch on iOS (jumpy or stuck mid-animation), so the workaround was to fade out → instantly reposition → fade in.

We don't actually know if iOS still has that bug — only an iPhone/iPad test would tell. If you want, I can:
- Leave it as-is (clean CSS transition for everyone) and revisit if you spot jank on iOS
- Add the iOS branch back but tighten the detection so it only triggers on real iPhone/iPad UAs, not Brave macOS

What's your call?

5	export function closeContent() {
6		const main = document.querySelector("main");
7		document.body.classList.remove("content-mode");
8		main.classList.remove("popup-scrollable");
9	
10		// Wait for fade-out transition to complete

1	import { initController, on } from "../controller/main.js";
2	import { initPopup } from "../popup/main.js";
3	import { FACES } from "../shared/faces.js";
4	import * as THREE from "../three.module.js";
5	import { animate, onResize } from "./animation.js";
6	import {
7		FACE_CANVAS_HEIGHT,
8		FACE_CANVAS_WIDTH,
9		GRAIN_INTENSITY_FACE,
10		GRAIN_SIZE_FACE,
11		GUIDANCE_DELAY_MS,
12		INITIAL_PYRAMID_ROTATION,
13	} from "./config.js";
14	import { buildPyramid } from "./geometry.js";
15	import {
16		$,
17		addGrainToCanvas,
18		getResponsiveCameraY,
19		getResponsiveCameraZ,
20		getResponsiveFOV,
21		getResponsiveLookAtY,
22	} from "./helpers.js";
23	import {
24		onKeyDown,
25		onPointerDown,
26		onPointerMove,
27		onPointerUp,
28		onShapeClick,
29	} from "./interaction.js";
30	import { loader, shapeState } from "./shapeState.js";
31	
32	/* ---------- init ---------------------------------------------------- */
33	export async function init() {
34		const container = document.createElement("div");
35		container.className = "three-box";
36		Object.assign(container.style, {
37			width: "100%",
38			height: "calc(100vh - 4rem)",
39			display: "block",
40			position: "relative",
41			opacity: "0",
42			transition: "opacity 1s ease-in-out",
43		});
44	
45		document.body.insertBefore(container, $("main"));
46	
47		// Create guidance element
48		const guidanceElement = document.createElement("div");
49		guidanceElement.className = "shape-guidance";
50		guidanceElement.innerHTML = `
51			<span class="guidance-drag">drag</span>
52			<span class="guidance-click">click</span>
53		`;
54		guidanceElement.style.opacity = "0";
55		container.appendChild(guidanceElement);
56		shapeState.guidanceElement = guidanceElement;
57	
58		shapeState.scene = new THREE.Scene();
59		shapeState.renderer = new THREE.WebGLRenderer({
60			antialias: true,
61			alpha: true,
62		});
63		container.appendChild(shapeState.renderer.domElement);
64	
65		// Create static grain texture
66		const grainCanvas = document.createElement("canvas");
67		grainCanvas.width = 512;
68		grainCanvas.height = 512;
69		const ctx = grainCanvas.getContext("2d");
70		ctx.fillStyle = "gray";
71		ctx.fillRect(0, 0, 512, 512);
72		addGrainToCanvas(grainCanvas, GRAIN_INTENSITY_FACE, GRAIN_SIZE_FACE);
73	
74		/* ---- create camera early ---- */
75		const cameraZ = getResponsiveCameraZ(window.innerWidth);
76		const cameraY = getResponsiveCameraY(window.innerWidth);
77		const lookAtY = getResponsiveLookAtY(window.innerWidth);
78		const fov = getResponsiveFOV(window.innerWidth);
79		shapeState.camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(
80			fov,
81			container.clientWidth / container.clientHeight,
82			0.1,
83			100,
84		);
85		shapeState.camera.position.set(0, cameraY, cameraZ);
86		shapeState.camera.lookAt(0, lookAtY, 0);
87	
88		// Add lighting for dynamic brightening effect
89		// Ambient light provides base illumination so faces aren't completely dark
90		const ambientLight = new THREE.AmbientLight(0xffffff, 0.7);
91		shapeState.scene.add(ambientLight);
92	
93		// Directional light creates dynamic brightening based on face angle
94		const directionalLight = new THREE.DirectionalLight(0xffffff, 2.5);
95		directionalLight.position.set(6, 4, 5);
96		shapeState.scene.add(directionalLight);
97	
98		// Load textures with error handling and fallback
99		const texturePromises = FACES.map((face) => {
100			return new Promise((resolve) => {
101				const onLoad = (texture) => {
102					texture.colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;
103					resolve(texture);
104				};
105				const onError = () => {
106					// Create fallback solid color texture
107					const canvas = document.createElement("canvas");
108					canvas.width = 256;
109					canvas.height = 256;
110					const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
111					ctx.fillStyle = "#504945";
112					ctx.fillRect(0, 0, 256, 256);
113					const fallbackTexture = new THREE.CanvasTexture(canvas);
114					fallbackTexture.colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;
115					resolve(fallbackTexture);
116				};
117				loader.load(face.image, onLoad, undefined, onError);
118			});
119		});
120		const loadedTextures = await Promise.all(texturePromises);
121	
122		shapeState.pyramid = buildPyramid(loadedTextures, grainCanvas);
123		shapeState.pyramid.rotation.set(
124			INITIAL_PYRAMID_ROTATION.x,
125			INITIAL_PYRAMID_ROTATION.y,
126			INITIAL_PYRAMID_ROTATION.z,
127		);
128		shapeState.scene.add(shapeState.pyramid);
129	
130		// Signal that shape is loaded and trigger intro completion
131		window.completeIntro(container);
132	
133		// Start guidance timer - show after configured delay if no popup opened
134		shapeState.guidanceTimer = setTimeout(() => {
135			if (!shapeState.hasOpenedPopup) {
136				showGuidance();
137			}
138		}, GUIDANCE_DELAY_MS);
139	
140		/* ---- events ---- */
```

> AGENT

Done. Flow:
1. While 3D scene is loading in background, `updateLoadProgress` ticks from 20 → 95 as the 5 face textures load
2. If user clicks "cool" before scene finishes → `loading-cool` class added → "Loading X%" overlays the center
3. When `completeIntro` fires → progress hits 100, overlay hides, 3D scene fades in

Reload, switch to simple, then click cool while scene is loading to test (you can throttle network in DevTools to make the 3D scene load slowly enough to see the percentage).

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01NsGbt1HpZUmNEPYgfAeRno
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/aether/services/sumeetsaini_com/index.html",
  "old_string": "  window._loadProgress = 0;\n  window.updateLoadProgress = function(pct) {\n    window._loadProgress = pct;\n    var el = document.getElementById('cool-loading-pct');\n    if (el) el.textContent = Math.round(pct);\n  };",
  "new_string": "  window._loadProgress = 0;\n  window._loadTarget = 0;\n  var _displayed = 0;\n  function renderProgress() {\n    var el = document.getElementById('cool-loading-pct');\n    if (el) el.textContent = Math.round(_displayed);\n  }\n  // Smoothly interpolate displayed progress toward target each frame.\n  (function tick() {\n    var diff = window._loadTarget - _displayed;\n    if (Math.abs(diff) > 0.05) {\n      _displayed += diff * 0.08;\n      window._loadProgress = _displayed;\n      renderProgress();\n    }\n    requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n  })();\n  // Fake creep up to 25% while three.js + shape.js are downloading and parsing\n  // (no real signal available during this window).\n  var _creep = setInterval(function() {\n    if (window._loadTarget < 25) {\n      window._loadTarget = Math.min(25, window._loadTarget + 0.6);\n    } else {\n      clearInterval(_creep);\n    }\n  }, 100);\n  window.updateLoadProgress = function(pct) {\n    if (pct > window._loadTarget) window._loadTarget = pct;\n    if (pct >= 100) {\n      window._loadTarget = 100;\n      // Snap to 100 instantly on completion\n      _displayed = 100;\n      window._loadProgress = 100;\n      renderProgress();\n    }\n  };"
}
```

264	function fadeOutCurrentContent() {
265		return new Promise((resolve) => {
266			const main = document.querySelector("main");
267			const allContent = Array.from(main.children).filter(
268				(child) => child.id !== "close-content",
269			);
270	
271			if (allContent.length === 0) {
272				resolve();
273				return;
274			}
275	
276			const fadeDuration = getComputedStyle(
277				document.documentElement,
278			).getPropertyValue("--content-switch-fade-duration");
279	
280			allContent.forEach((element) => {
281				element.style.transition = `opacity ${fadeDuration} ease-out`;
282				element.style.opacity = "0";
283			});
284	
285			setTimeout(() => {
286				resolve();
287			}, parseFloat(fadeDuration) * 1000);
288		});
289	}
290	
291	function animateContent() {
292		const main = document.querySelector("main");
293		const heading = main.querySelector("h2");
294		const otherElements = Array.from(main.children).filter(
295			(child) => child !== heading && child.id !== "close-content",
296		);
297	
298		if (heading) {
299			// Apply typewriter effect to heading
300			const text = heading.textContent;
301			heading.textContent = "";
302			heading.style.opacity = "1";
303	
304			let idx = 0;
305			const typeSpeed = parseInt(
306				getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue(
307					"--content-typewriter-speed",
308				),
309				10,
310			);
311			const typeInterval = setInterval(() => {
312				heading.textContent += text[idx++];
313				if (idx === text.length) {
314					clearInterval(typeInterval);
315	
316					// Fade in now selector immediately after typewriter completes
317					const selectorContainer = document.querySelector(".now-selector");
318					if (selectorContainer) {
319						selectorContainer.classList.add("loaded");
320					}
321	
322					// Add delay before fade-in starts
323					const fadeStartDelay = parseInt(
324						getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue(
325							"--content-fade-start-delay",
326						),
327						10,
328					);
329					setTimeout(() => {
330						fadeInElements(otherElements);
331					}, fadeStartDelay);
332				}
333			}, typeSpeed);
334		} else {
335			// If no heading, just fade in all elements
336			fadeInElements(otherElements);
337		}
338	}
339	
340	function fadeInElements(elements) {
341		const fadeDuration = getComputedStyle(
342			document.documentElement,
343		).getPropertyValue("--content-fade-duration");
344		const staggerDelay = parseInt(
345			getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue(
346				"--content-fade-stagger",
347			),
348			10,
349		);
350	
351		elements.forEach((element, index) => {
352			// Clear any existing inline opacity styles that might conflict
353			element.style.removeProperty("opacity");
354			element.style.removeProperty("transition");
355	
356			// Set initial state for animation
357			element.style.opacity = "0";
358			element.style.transition = `opacity ${fadeDuration} ease-in-out`;
359

230	function checkContentOverflow() {

239			if (contentHeight > availableHeight) {

241			} else {
242				main.classList.remove("popup-scrollable");
243			}
244		}

255			// Add switching class to fade out

> AGENT

A pretty rich snapshot — the HTML payload (≈1300 lines, ~50KB) is served the same to everyone, and an AI parser that doesn't run JS gets the no-JS fallback rendered. Specifically:

**SEO/metadata**
- `<title>` — "Sumeet Saini | Explorer, Developer, Creator"
- `<meta name="description">` + Open Graph tags
- JSON-LD structured data (Person + WebSite)

**Visible content (in `#fallback-content`)**
- H1 "Sumeet Saini" + an "About" intro paragraph linking to Reliq, GitHub, LinkedIn, CV
- A working `<form>` contact section that POSTs to `/vulkan/web_contact`
- **21 projects** in `<details><summary>` accordions — title, tech tags, description, "Visit Project" link
- **~15 blog posts** as a list of `<a>` links with titles and dates pointing to arcanecodex.dev
- **8 months of "Now" updates** (Oct 2025 → May 2026) with Focus paragraphs and Spotify embeds (inline `<iframe>`)
- Closing CTAs: "Check out my GitHub… or use the 'cool' version" and "Read the latest at arcanecodex.dev"

**What AI agents miss**
- The 3D pyramid, project images/videos, popup interactions
- Live data — the fallback is a static snapshot, so new projects/posts only appear after the file is regenerated

So for "summarize this person" / "what is this site about" / RAG-style ingestion, an agent gets the same factual substance a human reader gets — just without the visuals.

> DEVELOPER

can an AI agent ever get the live stuff? would it also get new now updates?

> AGENT

Two separate questions, two different answers:

**Live API data (projects + blog)**
- **JS-rendering crawlers** (Googlebot, Bing's renderer, some scraper agents like Bright Data, ScrapingBee in render mode): yes — they execute `home.js`, which hits the Vulkan and Arcane Codex APIs, so they get the live list.
- **Pure-HTML crawlers** (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot, most LLM training crawlers): no — they only see the hardcoded snapshot in `index.html`. They'd only get live data if they were smart enough to fetch `vulkan.sumeetsaini.com/projects/` and `arcanecodex.dev/index.json` directly. Some do follow JSON endpoints found in `preconnect` / `link` tags or robots.txt, but it's hit-and-miss.

**New "Now" updates**
- No, not without you (or a script) updating `index.html`. The 8 months currently in the fallback are hand-baked into the file. When you add June 2026, the no-JS crawler will still only see Oct 2025 → May 2026.

**If you want to fix this**, two practical options:
1. **Build step**: a tiny script (Node / Python / shell) that runs before each deploy, fetches the APIs + reads `content/now/*.html`, and regenerates the fallback markup inside `index.html`. Zero runtime cost, always fresh on each deploy.
2. **Server-side rendering** at the edge (Cloudflare Worker / similar): inject the live data into the HTML on each request. Higher cost/complexity, but truly live.

Option 1 is the high-leverage one — keeps the static-file simplicity, just automates the "now I need to manually update fallbacks" step.

```
// Now page loader - automatically loads the most recent month from content/now/

// Static list of available months - update this when adding new files
const AVAILABLE_MONTHS = [
	{ month: 10, year: 2025, filename: "10-2025.html" },
	{ month: 11, year: 2025, filename: "11-2025.html" },
	{ month: 12, year: 2025, filename: "12-2025.html" },
	{ month: 1, year: 2026, filename: "01-2026.html" },
	{ month: 2, year: 2026, filename: "02-2026.html" },
	{ month: 3, year: 2026, filename: "03-2026.html" },
	{ month: 4, year: 2026, filename: "04-2026.html" },
	{ month: 5, year: 2026, filename: "05-2026.html" },
	// Add new months here as they're created
];

export async function loadNowContent() {
	try {
		if (AVAILABLE_MONTHS.length === 0) {
			return "<h2>Now</h2><p>No now page content found.</p>";
		}

		// Find the most recent entry from static list
		const latestEntry = AVAILABLE_MONTHS.reduce((latest, current) => {
			const latestDate = new Date(latest.year, latest.month - 1);
			const currentDate = new Date(current.year, current.month - 1);
			return currentDate > latestDate ? current : latest;
		});

		// Load ONLY the most recent content immediately
		const content = await loadNowContentByMonth(
			latestEntry.month,
			latestEntry.year,
		);

		// Generate selector HTML using static list (no HTTP requests)
		const selectorHTML = generateSelectorHTML(
			latestEntry.month,
			latestEntry.year,
		);

		// Wrap with h2 and selector for consistency with other pages
		return `<h2>Now</h2>${selectorHTML}${content}`;
	} catch (error) {
		console.error("Error loading now content:", error);
		return "<h2>Now</h2><p>Could not load now page content.</p>";
	}
}

> AGENT

`nowLoader.js` is what powers the **cool version's** Now page — it reads from `content/now/*.html` files. But two important caveats:

**1. It's still partially manual.** Look at `js/now/nowLoader.js` lines 4-14 — there's a hardcoded `AVAILABLE_MONTHS` array. When you add a new month, you have to:
- Drop the new `06-2026.html` file into `content/now/`
- Add `{ month: 6, year: 2026, filename: "06-2026.html" }` to the `AVAILABLE_MONTHS` array

If you skip step 2, the cool version won't know the new month exists.

**2. It only runs in JS.** The nowLoader is JavaScript — it only executes when a browser (or JS-rendering crawler) opens the "now" face on the 3D scene. Pure-HTML AI crawlers never run it. They see whatever is hardcoded inside `#fallback-content` in `index.html`.

So today, when you add a new month, you actually have to update **three places**:
1. Create `content/now/<month>-<year>.html` (cool version reads this)
2. Add the entry to `AVAILABLE_MONTHS` in `nowLoader.js` (cool version's selector list)
3. Add the month's HTML + a radio button to the `#now` section in `index.html` (simple version + AI crawlers)

A build script could automate #2 and #3 by globbing `content/now/*.html` and regenerating both. Want me to write one?

name: Deploy to Aether

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]
  repository_dispatch:
    types: [arcanecodex-updated]

jobs:
  build-and-deploy:
    runs-on: ubuntu-24.04-arm
    permissions:
      contents: read
      packages: write

    steps:
    - name: Checkout code
      uses: actions/checkout@v4
      with:
        submodules: recursive
        token: ${{ secrets.VULKAN_GIT_TOKEN }}

    - name: Log in to GitHub Container Registry
      uses: docker/login-action@v3
      with:
        registry: ghcr.io
        username: ${{ github.actor }}
        password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}

    - name: Build and push Vulkan image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/vulkan
        file: ./services/vulkan/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/vulkan:latest

    - name: Build and push sumeetsaini_com image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/sumeetsaini_com
        file: ./services/sumeetsaini_com/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/sumeetsaini_com:latest

    - name: Build and push gateway image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/gateway
        file: ./services/gateway/Dockerfile
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/gateway:latest

    - name: Checkout latest arcanecodex content
      uses: actions/checkout@v4
      with:
        repository: kungfusaini/arcane-codex
        path: ./arcanecodex-latest
        token: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
        submodules: recursive

    - name: Build and push arcanecodex image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./arcanecodex-latest
        file: ./arcanecodex-latest/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/arcanecodex:latest

    - name: Build and push bucketbot image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/bucketbot
        file: ./services/bucketbot/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/bucketbot:latest

    - name: Build and push goblinbot image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/goblinbot
        file: ./services/goblinbot/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/goblinbot:latest

    - name: Build and push ao3 image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/ao3
        file: ./services/ao3/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/ao3:latest

    - name: Build and push reliqstudios image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/reliq/reliqstudios
        file: ./services/reliq/reliqstudios/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/reliqstudios:latest

    - name: Build and push reliqlabs image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/reliq/reliqlabs
        file: ./services/reliq/reliqlabs/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/reliqlabs:latest

    - name: Build and push reliqdigital image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/reliq/reliq.digital
        file: ./services/reliq/reliq.digital/Dockerfile.prod
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/reliqdigital:latest

    - name: Build and push card image
      uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
      with:
        context: ./services/card
        file: ./services/card/Dockerfile
        push: true
        platforms: linux/arm64
        tags: ghcr.io/kungfusaini/card.sumeetsaini.com:latest

    - name: Sync compose files to AETHER
      uses: appleboy/scp-action@v0.1.7
      with:
        host: ${{ secrets.AETHER_HOST }}
        username: ${{ secrets.AETHER_USER }}
        key: ${{ secrets.AETHER_SSH_KEY }}
        source: "docker-compose*.yml,scripts/"
        target: ${{ secrets.AETHER_PATH }}
        strip_components: 0
        overwrite: true

    - name: Deploy to AETHER
      uses: appleboy/ssh-action@v1.0.3
      with:
        host: ${{ secrets.AETHER_HOST }}
        username: ${{ secrets.AETHER_USER }}
        key: ${{ secrets.AETHER_SSH_KEY }}
        script: |
          cd ${{ secrets.AETHER_PATH }}
          
          # Create network if it doesn't exist
          docker network create aether-network 2>/dev/null || true
          
          # Pull new images
          MAIN_EMAIL=${{ secrets.MAIN_EMAIL }} \
          RELIQ_STUDIOS_EMAIL=${{ secrets.RELIQ_STUDIOS_EMAIL }} \
          PLAUSIBLE_SECRET_KEY_BASE=${{ secrets.PLAUSIBLE_SECRET_KEY_BASE }} \
          WELL_API_KEY=${{ secrets.WELL_API_KEY }} \
          BUCKETBOT_TOKEN=${{ secrets.BUCKETBOT_TOKEN }} \
          GOBLINBOT_TOKEN=${{ secrets.GOBLINBOT_TOKEN }} \
          VULKAN_GIT_TOKEN="${{ secrets.VULKAN_GIT_TOKEN }}" \
          PROJECTS_REPO_URL="${{ secrets.PROJECTS_REPO_URL }}" \
          PROJECTS_WEBHOOK_SECRET="${{ secrets.PROJECTS_WEBHOOK_SECRET }}" \
          TELEGRAM_ID="${{ secrets.TELEGRAM_ID }}" \
          docker compose -f docker-compose.yml \
                        -f docker-compose-prod.yml \
                        -f docker-compose-mailcow.yml \
                        -f docker-compose-plausible.yml \
                        pull

          # Rolling update (no "down", no "up --force-recreate")
          COMPOSE_FILES="-f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose-prod.yml -f docker-compose-mailcow.yml -f docker-compose-plausible.yml"
          export MAIN_EMAIL=${{ secrets.MAIN_EMAIL }}
          export RELIQ_STUDIOS_EMAIL=${{ secrets.RELIQ_STUDIOS_EMAIL }}
          export WELL_API_KEY=${{ secrets.WELL_API_KEY }}
          export PLAUSIBLE_SECRET_KEY_BASE=${{ secrets.PLAUSIBLE_SECRET_KEY_BASE }}
          export BUCKETBOT_TOKEN=${{ secrets.BUCKETBOT_TOKEN }}
          export GOBLINBOT_TOKEN=${{ secrets.GOBLINBOT_TOKEN }}
          export VULKAN_GIT_TOKEN="${{ secrets.VULKAN_GIT_TOKEN }}"
          export PLAUSIBLE_BASE_URL=https://stats.sumeetsaini.com
          export PLAUSIBLE_DISABLE_REGISTRATION=invite_only
          export VULKAN_GIT_TOKEN=${{ secrets.VULKAN_GIT_TOKEN }}
          export PROJECTS_REPO_URL=${{ secrets.PROJECTS_REPO_URL }}
          export PROJECTS_WEBHOOK_SECRET=${{ secrets.PROJECTS_WEBHOOK_SECRET }}
          export TELEGRAM_ID=${{ secrets.TELEGRAM_ID }}
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale plausible_db=1 plausible_db
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale plausible_events_db=1 plausible_events_db
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale plausible=1 plausible
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale gateway=1 gateway
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale vulkan=1 vulkan
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale sumeetsaini_com=1 sumeetsaini_com
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale arcanecodex=1 arcanecodex
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale bucketbot=1 bucketbot
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale goblinbot=1 goblinbot
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale ao3=1 ao3
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale reliqstudios=1 reliqstudios
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale reliqlabs=1 reliqlabs
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale reliqdigital=1 reliqdigital
          docker compose $COMPOSE_FILES up -d --no-deps --scale card=1 card
           
           # Clean up old images
          docker image prune -f
```

> DEVELOPER

Well, let's talk about it first before you make any changes.

> AGENT

Yeah, makes sense — bunch of decisions here. Let me lay out what I was going to do so we can poke at it:

**The script**
A Node ES-module script in `scripts/build-fallback.mjs` that:
- Reads `content/now/*.html` → sorts by date → generates the now section + month radios + the per-month CSS selector rules
- Fetches `vulkan.sumeetsaini.com/projects/` → generates the `<details>` accordion
- Fetches `arcanecodex.dev/index.json` → generates the recent posts `<ul>`
- Reads `index.html`, replaces content between marker comments, writes back

Markers in `index.html`:
```html
<!-- BEGIN:fallback-now-section --> ... <!-- END:fallback-now-section -->
<!-- BEGIN:fallback-now-radios --> ... <!-- END:fallback-now-radios -->
<!-- BEGIN:fallback-projects --> ... <!-- END:fallback-projects -->
<!-- BEGIN:fallback-blog --> ... <!-- END:fallback-blog -->
```
Plus `/* BEGIN:fallback-now-css */ ... /* END:fallback-now-css */` inside the inline `<style>` for the per-month `:has` rules.

**Things worth deciding before I start**

1. **Now file format.** Today, `content/now/05-2026.html` is just raw paragraphs and a Spotify embed — no `<div>` wrapper, no date label. Meanwhile the current fallback has `<p class="now-date">May 2026</p>` and `<h4>Focus</h4>` headings that aren't in the source files. Options:
   - Script adds the date label automatically. You'd put any `<h4>Focus</h4>` in the source file yourself (going forward).
   - Or change nothing and just match the file exactly — fallback won't have date labels.

2. **Where the script runs.** Two viable spots:
   - **CI step in `deploy.yml` before docker build** — runs on GitHub's runner, commits the regenerated `index.html` back to main, then Docker just copies the fresh file. Pro: single source of truth in git. Con: CI needs commit permissions, and you get a CI-authored commit on every change.
   - **Inside `Dockerfile.prod` as a multi-stage build** — node stage runs script, nginx stage serves the result. Pro: zero extra commits, anyone can `docker build` and get fresh fallback. Con: every deploy hits the APIs even if nothing changed.

3. **Projects-updated trigger.** Today `deploy.yml` runs on push to main and on `repository_dispatch: arcanecodex-updated`. For projects, I'd add `projects-updated` to the dispatch types — but you'd need to wire your projects repo (or Vulkan's webhook handler) to actually fire that event. Do you know if that already exists, or should I just add a daily cron as a fallback?

4. **Blog snapshot size.** Arcane Codex has 15 posts now. Do you want the fallback to keep showing all of them, or cap at the latest N (say 10) to keep `index.html` from growing unbounded?

5. **The `content/now/*.html` file convention going forward.** Right now nowLoader expects just `MM-YYYY.html` raw content. If I add the date label automatically, you keep writing files exactly as you do now — no behavior change needed. ✅ Just confirming you're OK with that.

Want to lock in (1) and (2) first?

{
  "file_path": "/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/aether/services/sumeetsaini_com/scripts/build-fallback.mjs",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Regenerates the no-JS fallback sections inside index.html from live sources:\n//   - content/now/*.html  → now section + radios + per-month CSS\n//   - vulkan.sumeetsaini.com/projects/  → projects accordion\n//   - arcanecodex.dev/index.json  → recent blog posts\n//\n// Run with: npm run build-fallback\n\nimport { readFile, writeFile, readdir } from \"node:fs/promises\";\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \"node:url\";\nimport { dirname, join } from \"node:path\";\n\nconst __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));\nconst ROOT = join(__dirname, \"..\");\nconst INDEX_PATH = join(ROOT, \"index.html\");\nconst NOW_DIR = join(ROOT, \"content\", \"now\");\n\nconst PROJECTS_URL = \"https://vulkan.sumeetsaini.com/projects/\";\nconst BLOG_URL = \"https://arcanecodex.dev/index.json\";\n\nconst MONTH_NAMES = [\n\t\"January\", \"February\", \"March\", \"April\", \"May\", \"June\",\n\t\"July\", \"August\", \"September\", \"October\", \"November\", \"December\",\n];\nconst MONTH_SHORT = [\n\t\"Jan\", \"Feb\", \"Mar\", \"Apr\", \"May\", \"Jun\",\n\t\"Jul\", \"Aug\", \"Sep\", \"Oct\", \"Nov\", \"Dec\",\n];\n\nfunction escapeHtml(s) {\n\treturn String(s ?? \"\")\n\t\t.replace(/&/g, \"&amp;\")\n\t\t.replace(/</g, \"&lt;\")\n\t\t.replace(/>/g, \"&gt;\")\n\t\t.replace(/\"/g, \"&quot;\");\n}\n\n// ---------- NOW ----------\nasync function buildNow() {\n\tconst files = (await readdir(NOW_DIR)).filter((f) => /^\\d{2}-\\d{4}\\.html$/.test(f));\n\tconst entries = await Promise.all(\n\t\tfiles.map(async (filename) => {\n\t\t\tconst [mm, yyyy] = filename.replace(\".html\", \"\").split(\"-\").map(Number);\n\t\t\tconst content = await readFile(join(NOW_DIR, filename), \"utf8\");\n\t\t\treturn { month: mm, year: yyyy, content: content.trimEnd() };\n\t\t}),\n\t);\n\t// Sort chronologically (oldest → newest), matches existing fallback order.\n\tentries.sort((a, b) => (a.year - b.year) || (a.month - b.month));\n\n\tconst latest = entries[entries.length - 1];\n\tconst id = (e) => `sel-now-${e.year}-${String(e.month).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n\tconst contentId = (e) => `now-${e.year}-${String(e.month).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n\n\tconst radios = entries\n\t\t.map((e) => {\n\t\t\tconst checked = e === latest ? \" checked\" : \"\";\n\t\t\treturn `      <input type=\"radio\" name=\"now-month\" id=\"${id(e)}\" class=\"now-month-selector\"${checked}>`;\n\t\t})\n\t\t.join(\"\\n\");\n\n\t// Per-month CSS — one block for active-label color, one for content display.\n\tconst labelRules = entries\n\t\t.map((e) => `    #fallback-content:has(#${id(e)}:checked) .now-month-nav label[for=\"${id(e)}\"]`)\n\t\t.join(\",\\n\");\n\tconst contentRules = entries\n\t\t.map((e) => `    #fallback-content:has(#${id(e)}:checked) #${contentId(e)}`)\n\t\t.join(\",\\n\");\n\tconst css = `${labelRules} {\n      color: var(--cream);\n    }\n${contentRules} {\n      display: block;\n    }`;\n\n\tconst monthDivs = entries\n\t\t.map((e) => {\n\t\t\tconst label = `${MONTH_SHORT[e.month - 1]} ${e.year}`;\n\t\t\treturn `<div id=\"${contentId(e)}\" class=\"now-month-content\">\n<p class=\"now-date\">${label}</p>\n${e.content}\n</div>`;\n\t\t})\n\t\t.join(\"\\n\\n\");\n\n\tconst navLabels = entries\n\t\t.map((e) => `<label for=\"${id(e)}\">${MONTH_SHORT[e.month - 1]} ${e.year}</label>`)\n\t\t.join(\"\\n\");\n\tconst nav = `<div class=\"now-month-nav\">\\n${navLabels}\\n</div>`;\n\n\tconst section = `${monthDivs}\\n\\n${nav}`;\n\n\treturn { radios, css, section };\n}\n\n// ---------- PROJECTS ----------\nasync function buildProjects() {\n\tconst res = await fetch(PROJECTS_URL);\n\tif (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Projects API ${res.status}`);\n\tconst data = await res.json();\n\tconst projects = (data.projects || []).filter((p) => !p.draft);\n\n\treturn projects\n\t\t.map((p) => {\n\t\t\tconst title = escapeHtml(p.title);\n\t\t\tconst tech = (p.tech || []).map((t) => `<code>${escapeHtml(t)}</code>`).join(\" \");\n\t\t\t// Prefer site-specific copy if present, fall back to short description.\n\t\t\tconst longText = p.text?.sumeetsaini || p.description || \"\";\n\t\t\t// Strip markdown-ish stuff lightly: keep the first paragraph.\n\t\t\tconst desc = escapeHtml(longText.split(/\\n\\s*\\n/)[0].trim());\n\t\t\tconst link = p.link\n\t\t\t\t? `<p><a href=\"${escapeHtml(p.link)}\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\">Visit Project &rarr;</a></p>`\n\t\t\t\t: \"\";\n\t\t\treturn `<details>\n  <summary><strong>${title}</strong>${tech ? \" \" + tech : \"\"}</summary>\n  <p>${desc}</p>${link ? \"\\n  \" + link : \"\"}\n</details>`;\n\t\t})\n\t\t.join(\"\\n\");\n}\n\n// ---------- BLOG ----------\nasync function buildBlog() {\n\tconst res = await fetch(BLOG_URL);\n\tif (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Blog API ${res.status}`);\n\tconst posts = await res.json();\n\t// Sort newest first.\n\tposts.sort((a, b) => new Date(b.date) - new Date(a.date));\n\n\tconst items = posts\n\t\t.map((p) => {\n\t\t\tconst d = new Date(p.date);\n\t\t\tconst dateLabel = `${MONTH_SHORT[d.getUTCMonth()]} ${d.getUTCFullYear()}`;\n\t\t\tconst href = p.permalink || p.url;\n\t\t\treturn `<li><a href=\"${escapeHtml(href)}\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\">${escapeHtml(p.title)}</a> &mdash; ${dateLabel}</li>`;\n\t\t})\n\t\t.join(\"\\n\");\n\n\treturn `<ul>\\n${items}\\n</ul>`;\n}\n\n// ---------- replace markers ----------\nfunction replaceBlock(src, marker, replacement) {\n\tconst open = marker.openHtml ?? `<!-- BEGIN:${marker.name} -->`;\n\tconst close = marker.closeHtml ?? `<!-- END:${marker.name} -->`;\n\tconst re = new RegExp(\n\t\t`${escapeRegex(open)}[\\\\s\\\\S]*?${escapeRegex(close)}`,\n\t\t\"m\",\n\t);\n\tif (!re.test(src)) {\n\t\tthrow new Error(`Marker pair not found for: ${marker.name}`);\n\t}\n\treturn src.replace(re, `${open}\\n${replacement}\\n${close}`);\n}\n\nfunction escapeRegex(s) {\n\treturn s.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\]/g, \"\\\\$&\");\n}\n\n// ---------- main ----------\nasync function main() {\n\tconst [now, projects, blog] = await Promise.all([\n\t\tbuildNow(),\n\t\tbuildProjects(),\n\t\tbuildBlog(),\n\t]);\n\n\tlet html = await readFile(INDEX_PATH, \"utf8\");\n\n\thtml = replaceBlock(html, { name: \"fallback-now-css\", openHtml: \"/* BEGIN:fallback-now-css */\", closeHtml: \"/* END:fallback-now-css */\" }, now.css);\n\thtml = replaceBlock(html, { name: \"fallback-now-radios\" }, now.radios);\n\thtml = replaceBlock(html, { name: \"fallback-now-section\" }, now.section);\n\thtml = replaceBlock(html, { name: \"fallback-projects\" }, projects);\n\thtml = replaceBlock(html, { name: \"fallback-blog\" }, blog);\n\n\tawait writeFile(INDEX_PATH, html);\n\tconsole.log(`✓ Rebuilt fallback in index.html`);\n\tconsole.log(`  now:      ${(await readdir(NOW_DIR)).filter((f) => /^\\d{2}-\\d{4}\\.html$/.test(f)).length} months`);\n\tconsole.log(`  projects: ${(projects.match(/<details>/g) || []).length} entries`);\n\tconsole.log(`  blog:     ${(blog.match(/<li>/g) || []).length} posts`);\n}\n\nmain().catch((err) => {\n\tconsole.error(\"build-fallback failed:\", err);\n\tprocess.exit(1);\n});\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

okay so can you image you are an Ai agent and you use the websearch tool or something to find my website, would you find live stuff or what/

> AGENT

Honestly — they get a **snapshot, not live**.

Here's the actual flow:

1. AI agent does a search → gets your URL + a stale snippet (the search engine indexed it whenever it last crawled, could be days/weeks ago).
2. AI fetches the URL fresh with its web tool → gets the current `index.html` served by nginx.
3. They see whatever was baked in at the **last time you ran `npm run build-fallback` and deployed**. They don't execute JS, so they never hit the live APIs themselves.

So right now, after I just ran the script, an AI agent would see:
- All 21 projects from Vulkan
- 16 blog posts including the latest one
- The Oct 2025 → May 2026 Now updates

But the moment you add `06-2026.html` to `content/now/`, or post a new blog, or push a new project to Vulkan — they'll keep seeing the old snapshot until you `npm run build-fallback` + deploy again.

**Caveats / nuances**:
- The HTML has `<link rel="preconnect" href="https://vulkan.sumeetsaini.com">` and `https://arcanecodex.dev` — a *very* smart agent could follow those hints and hit the JSON APIs directly. Most won't bother.
- A JS-rendering crawler (Googlebot, etc.) would execute `home.js` on click and get live data, but they generally don't simulate clicks on a 3D scene.
- For LLM-context agents (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot doing training/grounding crawls), they almost always pull static HTML only.

**Practical answer to your question**: "Live-ish, as of your last build." Until you wire up the auto-regenerate-on-update CI step we discussed, the fallback is as fresh as your last manual run of the script.

Want me to commit what we have so far?

how do I run the script ?

From inside the `services/sumeetsaini_com` directory:

npm run build-fallback

- Reads `content/now/*.html`
- Hits `vulkan.sumeetsaini.com/projects/` and `arcanecodex.dev/index.json`
- Rewrites the marked sections inside `index.html`
- Prints a summary (e.g. `now: 8 months, projects: 21 entries, blog: 16 posts`)

After running it, `git diff index.html` to see what changed, then commit + push to deploy.

/* ---------- contact state management ---------- */
const contactState = {
	hasSubmitted: false,
	submissionData: null,
};

export function getContactState() {
	return contactState;
}

export function setContactState(state) {
	Object.assign(contactState, state);
}

/* ---------- contact form handling ---------- */
export function setupContactForm() {
	const contactForm = document.getElementById("contactForm");
	if (!contactForm) return;

	const resultDiv = document.getElementById("result");
	const submitButton = contactForm.querySelector('button[type="submit"]');

	// Helper function to set consistent message styling
	const setMessageStyle = () => {
		resultDiv.style.display = "block";
		resultDiv.style.background = "transparent";
		resultDiv.style.color = "var(--orange)";
		resultDiv.style.border = "none";
		resultDiv.style.fontSize = "0.8rem";
	};

	contactForm.addEventListener("submit", async (e) => {
		e.preventDefault();

		// Show loading state
		submitButton.textContent = "Sending...";
		submitButton.disabled = true;
		setMessageStyle();
		resultDiv.textContent = "Sending message...";

		const formData = new FormData(e.target);
		// Add origin field to identify which site the submission is from

			const res = await fetch("/vulkan/web_contact", {
				method: "POST",
				headers: { "Content-Type": "application/x-www-form-urlencoded" },
				body,

> AGENT

Found it — the cool version sends `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, but my script was sending `multipart/form-data` (default with `FormData` in `fetch`). Vulkan probably only handles the urlencoded variant.
